### 1047. Legislating Literacy at the State Level: Six Considerations for Supporting Students With Disabilities

```yaml
url: "https://www.wested.org/resource/legislating-literacy-at-the-state-level/"
type: report
source: "WestEd"
url_confirmed: true
description_inferred: false
description_source: llm-summary
date_added: 2026-05-10
doi: null
license: null
tags: [inclusive-design, k-12, literacy, response-to-intervention]
```

This WestEd Perspectives brief examines how states can better support students with disabilities through literacy policymaking. It outlines recent legislation affecting both general and special education systems and offers six key considerations for implementation. The brief addresses the intersection of structured literacy mandates with special education law, providing a resource list for policy development that helps states ensure literacy reforms reach all learners, including those with learning disabilities. Published 2025.

### 1048. From AI Risk to AI Readiness

```yaml
doi: null
license: null
tags: [ai-ethics, ai-policy, digital-learning-platforms, genai, k-12, pii-privacy]
```

This recorded webinar explores how educational leaders can assess their organization's readiness for AI implementation. The session covers "AI Readiness Assessment," fine-tuning approaches for large language models, privacy and governance considerations, and cost analysis for various implementation strategies. Experts Sarah Quesen and Baron Rodriguez provide frameworks for responsible AI integration while protecting student data and ensuring equitable access for students and staff. Published January 15, 2026.

### 1050. The Impact of NBA Math Hoops on Students' Math Achievement: A Randomized Controlled Trial

```yaml
url: "https://www.wested.org/resource/the-impact-of-nba-math-hoops-on-students-math-achievement/"
type: report
source: "WestEd"
url_confirmed: true
description_inferred: false
description_source: llm-summary
date_added: 2026-05-10
doi: null
license: null
tags: [digital-learning-platforms, k-12, math-education, math-motivation, rct]
```

This study evaluated how NBA Math Hoops affects student math performance through a rigorous experimental design. The research found that "students in NBA Math Hoops classrooms scored an average of 0.19 standard deviations higher than peers in STEM enrichment classrooms" — roughly equivalent to advancing from the 50th to 58th percentile. The program demonstrated practical benefits for educators, requiring minimal training and costing under $20 per student while remaining straightforward to implement in out-of-school settings. Published 2026.

---

### 1051. Advancing Achievement Through Biliteracy: Statewide Trends in Bilingual Teacher Supply, Demand, and Assignment in California

```yaml
url: "https://www.wested.org/resource/advancing-achievement-through-biliteracy-brief-1/"
type: report
source: "WestEd"
url_confirmed: true
description_inferred: false
description_source: llm-summary
date_added: 2026-05-10
doi: null
license: null
tags: [english-learners, k-12, longitudinal, professional-development]
```

This brief examines bilingual authorized (BLA) teacher workforce supply and demand trends in California using statewide data. The authors find that while the state has progressed toward its goal of 2,000 new bilingual authorizations annually, significant data gaps exist. The brief emphasizes the need for "improved data systems, strategic planning, and pathway investment" to support California's vision of universal bilingualism and biliteracy and ensure adequate bilingual teacher supply to serve the state's large English learner population. Published 2026.

---

### 1052. Friction by Design: A Framework for Centering Learning in the Age of AI

```yaml
url: "https://www.wested.org/resource/friction-by-design/"
type: framework
source: "WestEd"
url_confirmed: true
description_inferred: false
description_source: llm-summary
date_added: 2026-05-10
doi: null
license: null
tags: [ai-ethics, digital-learning-platforms, genai, k-12, personalized-learning]
```

This framework helps educators integrate AI intentionally while preserving meaningful learning. It distinguishes between productive friction — the effort and struggle that enables growth — and unproductive friction that obstructs progress. The guide employs five design lenses (Cognitive Ownership, Productive Struggle, Social Sense-Making, Activation Energy, and Access Barriers) to show how AI can reduce barriers while maintaining the challenge essential to learning. Published 2025.

---

### 1054. DISC AI Resource Collection

```yaml
url: "https://www.wested.org/resource/disc-ai-resource-collection/"
type: framework
source: "WestEd"
url_confirmed: true
description_inferred: false
description_source: llm-summary
date_added: 2026-05-10
doi: null
license: null
tags: [ai-ethics, ai-policy, data-sharing, pii-privacy]
```

This collection comprises practical guidance developed by WestEd's Data Integration Support Center for secure AI implementation in public agencies. The materials address the complete lifecycle of AI deployment, including "infrastructure requirements, technical methodologies, policy frameworks, and practical decision-making tools" to help organizations build trustworthy AI capabilities while protecting sensitive data. Designed for education agencies navigating AI adoption decisions, the collection covers responsible data governance, vendor evaluation, and privacy safeguards. Published 2024–2025.

### 1056. Youth Mental Health Corps Implementation Reporting: Resource Collection

```yaml
url: "https://www.wested.org/resource/youth-mental-health-corps-resource-collection/"
type: report
source: "WestEd"
url_confirmed: true
description_inferred: false
description_source: llm-summary
date_added: 2026-05-10
doi: null
license: null
tags: [career-readiness, college-access, k-12, sel]
```

This collection documents WestEd's evaluation of the Youth Mental Health Corps, a multisector partnership launched in 2024. The materials include a Year 1 report showing how the initiative "engaged 317 members across four states, reached thousands of students and families," plus four state case studies from Colorado, Michigan, Minnesota, and Texas. The resources examine how near-peer navigators expand youth mental health support while building the behavioral health workforce, offering a model for scaling mental health services in school-linked contexts. Published September 2025.

### 1057. What Is The Statewide BHSSA Evaluation?

```yaml
url: "https://www.wested.org/resource/what-is-the-statewide-bhssa-evaluation/"
type: report
source: "WestEd"
url_confirmed: true
description_inferred: false
description_source: llm-summary
date_added: 2026-05-10
doi: null
license: null
tags: [k-12, longitudinal, sel]
```

This fact sheet explains California's Behavioral Health Student Services Act (BHSSA) evaluation, which funds partnerships between county behavioral health departments and schools. WestEd and the Commission for Behavioral Health are leading the assessment "from July 2025 through June 2027" using five evaluation components to measure how BHSSA funds have been deployed and their impact on school behavioral health systems. The evaluation will track implementation quality, reach, and outcomes across California school districts. Published 2026.

### 1058. Youth Reimagining School Behavioral Health Systems

```yaml
url: "https://www.wested.org/resource/youth-reimagining-school-behavioral-health-systems/"
type: report
source: "WestEd"
url_confirmed: true
description_inferred: false
description_source: llm-summary
date_added: 2026-05-10
doi: null
license: null
tags: [k-12, sel, student-belonging]
```

This resource outlines the Youth Engagement Cohort (YEC) model, "a five-session, student-centered process" where students analyze school data, reflect on available supports, and present recommendations to school leaders. The initiative ensures young people shape California's behavioral health transformation efforts while developing leadership and evaluation competencies. The model positions students as active researchers and advocates within their school communities, contributing to California's broader BHSSA implementation goals. Published 2026.

### 1059. The California State University's AANHPI Student Achievement Program: An Evaluation of Progress From Spring 2024 Through Fall 2025

```yaml
url: "https://www.wested.org/resource/the-california-state-universitys-asian-american-native-hawaiian-and-pacific-islander-student-achievement-program/"
type: report
source: "WestEd"
url_confirmed: true
description_inferred: false
description_source: llm-summary
date_added: 2026-05-10
doi: null
license: null
tags: [college-access, higher-ed, longitudinal, student-belonging]
```

This WestEd evaluation examined how legislative funding was distributed across all 23 CSU campuses and its initial effects on AANHPI students. The report found that "ASAP-supported initiatives strengthened students' academic engagement, personal well-being, and connection to AANHPI cultures and histories." Survey results indicated participating students reported notably more positive views regarding campus climate, belonging, and self-efficacy compared to non-participants. However, implementation showed considerable variation among campuses due to differences in institutional support history, staffing, student demographics, and data systems. Published March 2026.

---

A research brief that responds to a widely covered critique claiming educational research has become disconnected from classroom realities. ### 6321. How Are Suspensions Related to School Climate in California Middle Schools?

```yaml
url: "https://www.wested.org/resource/suspensions-and-school-climate/"
type: report
source: "WestEd"
url_confirmed: true
description_inferred: false
description_source: listing
date_added: 2026-08-30
doi: null
license: null
tags: []
```

This report explores how recent changes in suspension rates at middle schools in California relate to changes in school climate. It uses the state’s data on out-of-school suspension rates and California Healthy Kids Survey data on school climate. Overall, the findings indicate that school climate improved most in schools that also experienced the greatest declines in out-of-school suspension rates. There was no evidence to support concerns that reducing suspension rates would also reduce school safety.

---

---

```yaml
url: "https://www.wested.org/resource/formal-system-processing-of-juveniles-effects-on-delinquency/"
type: report
source: "WestEd"
url_confirmed: true
description_inferred: false
description_source: listing
date_added: 2026-08-30
doi: null
license: null
tags: []
```

The objective of this paper is to answer an important policy question: Does juvenile system processing reduce subsequent delinquency? Cowritten by WestEd's Anthony Petrosino and Sarah Guckenburg with Carolyn Turpin-Petrosino for the Campbell Collaboration, it presents the background, objectives, methodology, and results of this systematic review, as well as a list of references and an appendix.

### 6741. Nurturing the Nurturers: The Importance of Sound Relationships in Early Childhood Intervention

```yaml
url: "https://www.wested.org/resource/nurturing-the-nurturers-the-importance-of-sound-relationships-in-early-childhood-intervention/"
type: report
source: "WestEd"
url_confirmed: true
description_inferred: false
description_source: listing
date_added: 2026-08-30
doi: null
license: null
tags: [early-childhood, response-to-intervention]
```

Sound relationships are the essence of Marin City Families First, an early-intervention model designed by WestEd’s Center for Child and Family Studies to ensure the health and well-being of children in low-income communities. The report describes how home visitors support client families and how, in turn, home visitors receive support from the program supervisor. A case study illustrates how these supportive relationships play out with one family.
